- **chorus-core**: Shared substrate for the chorus suite — the gate / decision / exploratory primitives, the conductor discipline, the single I1–I9 invariant catalog, and the record tools (JSON schema, validator, renderer, port bindings). When OpenSpec is not the resolved mode, this skill no-ops and the caller follows the resolved mode (spec-lite or free-form). - **chosetime**: Worker ritual to pick a queued task from `## TODO` (or `## BACKLOG`) and promote it to `## DOING` via kanbanger `move_task`. Writes `&lt;cwd&gt;/.claude/active-task-&lt;shortId&gt;.txt` so the Stop hook starts producing handoffs for the chosen task. Requires an open PM session (`&lt;cwd&gt;/.claude/pm-session-&lt;shortId&gt;.txt`) and at least one task queued via `/queuetime` (or any prior kanbanger action). Use when starting work on a previously-queued task. Companion to `/queuetime` (PM queues), `/worktime` (Worker spawns fresh, no queue stage), `/notetime` (mid-task notes), and `/clocktime` (close Worker task). ([earlyprototype/early-prototype](https://github.com/earlyprototype/early-prototype/tree/HEAD/plugins/early-prototype/skills/chosetime))

- **chroma-mcp-env-file-ignored-set-chroma-ce0657203a328ec5**: Fixes the Chroma MCP server ignoring environment variables because the dotenv file is not loaded. The fix is setting CHROMA_DOTENV_PATH to the absolute file path. Use when env vars work in the shell but not under the MCP client; not for wrong values. - **chromatin-contact-based-scoring**: Use when candidate enhancer regions need enhancer-gene interaction scores that incorporate chromatin contact data (e.g. Hi-C maps), such as ranking CRISPRi-validated enhancer-gene pairs on the three validation datasets or comparing against model-based scores like Enformer in-silico mutagenesis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-contact-based-scoring))
- **chromatin-contact-domain-calling**: Use when ChIA-PIPE loop-calling output (PET clusters with coordinates, PET counts, and anchor peak-support flags) from CTCF ChIA-PET data must be converted into chromatin contact domain (CCD) intervals. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-contact-domain-calling))
- **chromatin-contact-map-analysis**: Use when you have an intra-chromosomal NxN Hi-C contact matrix (e.g., KR-normalized at 50/25/5 Kb from GM12878) and need unsupervised TAD boundary detection without complex parameter tuning. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-contact-map-analysis))
- **chromatin-contact-matrix-generation**: Use when you hold a deduplicated ChIA-PET bedpe (e.g., in.rmDup.bedpe) and need a fixed-binsize Hi-C-style contact matrix (e.g., 10 kb, triplet sparse) to overlay with Hi-C maps or interrogate loop structure at anchors. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-contact-matrix-generation))
- **chromatin-contact-significance-calling**: Use when normalized Hi-C or capture Hi-C fragment-pair data are available and you need per-contact significance scores, reproducibility fractions between biological replicates, or a ROC AUC comparison against Fit-Hi-C. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-contact-significance-calling))
- **chromatin-contact-table-processing**: Use when Hi-C or capture Hi-C fragment-pair tables have already been coverage-normalized (bias-removed residuals with freq and dist columns) and the goal is to remove the distance-dependent decay background to obtain normalized interaction frequencies or call significant interactions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-contact-table-processing))
- **chromatin-domain-boundary-detection**: Use when you have generated a .hic contact map from Hi-C raw sequencing data and need to identify topologically associating domains (TADs) or other chromatin structural boundaries. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v1/skills/chromatin-domain-boundary-detection))
- **chromatin-domain-modularity-assessment**: Use when you need to evaluate or rank TLD/TAD predictions from a Hi-C matrix (bulk or single-cell) by 2D structural entropy and modularity indices, e.g., to confirm a predictor's first-class performance beyond AMI/WS benchmarks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-domain-modularity-assessment))
- **chromatin-factor-wt-vs-knockout-signal-comparison**: Use when comparing ChIP-seq signal of a chromatin regulator (e.g. MLL2) or its histone mark (e.g. H3K4me3) between WT and genetic-knockout samples, and you need depth-normalized ChIP levels plus averaged peak-centered meta-plots and boxplots. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-factor-wt-vs-knockout-signal-comparison))
- **chromatin-feature-prediction-model-training**: Use when the goal is to pre-train a sequence-based convolutional network on chromatin feature datasets (e.g. ENCODE/Roadmap marks such as CTCF, DNase, ChIP-seq) as the first phase of deepC transfer learning for chromatin interaction prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-feature-prediction-model-training))
- **chromatin-interaction-data-processing**: Use when raw paired-end FASTQ from ChIA-PET, HiChIP, or PLAC-seq libraries must be processed end-to-end into peaks, loops, chromatin contact domains, and 2D contact maps viewable in Juicebox/HiGlass. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-interaction-data-processing))
- **chromatin-interaction-loop-calling**: Use when processing ChIA-PET, HiChIP, or PLAC-seq read pairs (short 2×21 bp or long 2×150 bp) and you need to call chromatin interaction loops with peak-supported anchors from uniquely aligned, nonredundant PETs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-interaction-loop-calling))
- **chromatin-interaction-pet-preprocessing**: Use when raw paired-end ChIA-PET FASTQ reads must be scanned for bridge linker sequences and partitioned by linker status prior to read mapping and loop calling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-interaction-pet-preprocessing))
- **chromatin-interaction-prediction-evaluation**: Use when assessing how well sequence-based 3D genome folding predictions (deepC) reproduce observed Hi-C contacts on holdout chromosomes, e.g. computing Pearson correlations for raw vs smoothed maps or low-depth training sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-interaction-prediction-evaluation))
- **chromatin-interaction-sindel-annotation**: Use when validating that predicted noncoding indels are biologically meaningful by testing their enrichment in enhancer/promoter chromatin interactions or histone-mark regions against true cis-eQTL sindels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-interaction-sindel-annotation))
- **chromatin-loop-anchor-resolution**: Use when ChIA-PET reads must be resolved to allele-specific loops for a sample with genome-wide SNP phasing (e.g., NA12878), and you need statistically biased SNPs at loop anchors. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-loop-anchor-resolution))
- **chromatin-loop-calling-from-imaging-data**: Use when the input is per-cell, per-allele 3D coordinates of genomic loci from multiplexed DNA FISH or DNA seqFISH+ (e.g., 25 Kb or 5 Kb bins) and the goal is de novo chromatin loop detection and benchmarking against HiCCUPS references. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-loop-calling-from-imaging-data))
- **chromatin-loop-calling-from-schic**: Use when you have per-cell (tab-separated, optionally gzipped) scHi-C contact files for mm10/hg19/hg38 and need cell-type-specific chromatin loop candidates and summits. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-loop-calling-from-schic))
- **chromatin-loop-calling-statistical-testing**: Use when input is per-cell scHi-C or sn-m3C-seq contact files (e.g. 2,869 human prefrontal cortex sn-m3C-seq cells or 742 mouse ESCs) and the goal is to call cell-type-specific chromatin loops at 10-kb resolution. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-loop-calling-statistical-testing))
- **chromatin-loop-prediction-from-contact-maps**: Use when you have a 10 kb-binned .cool/.hic contact map and a positive interaction set (ChIA-PET/HiChIP/Capture Hi-C bedpe) and need supervised, data-driven chromatin loop calls that are robust to low sequencing depth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-loop-prediction-from-contact-maps))
- **chromatin-loop-transitivity-and-redundancy-analysis**: Use when a Hi-C contact map implies a transitive or redundant chromatin loop between loci and a ChIA-PET2 CTCF bedpe/contact matrix is available to confirm or refute the direct interaction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-loop-transitivity-and-redundancy-analysis))
- **chromatin-modeling-benchmark-comparison**: Use when two chromatin modelling tools must be compared for computational performance or run-to-run stability, and per-run execution times are available across repeated simulations on defined chromosomes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-modeling-benchmark-comparison))
- **chromatin-ndr-dynamics-visualization**: Use when comparing timepoint-specific nucleosome dyad positions (e.g., 0 min vs 60 min cadmium MNase-seq) across promoter-aligned gene groups to detect +1 nucleosome shifts and nucleosome-depleted region remodeling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-ndr-dynamics-visualization))
- **chromatin-peak-calling**: Use when processing ChIA-PET (or HiChIP/PLAC-seq) libraries and protein-factor binding peaks must be called from self-ligation PETs to support loop calling, CCD calling, and enhancer-promoter annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-peak-calling))
- **chromatin-profile-score-interpretation**: Use when a set of query noncoding SNPs must be functionally screened for their impact on chromatin accessibility, histone marks, or TF binding across many ENCODE/Roadmap epigenomic profiles in a tissue- or cell type-specific manner. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-profile-score-interpretation))
- **chromatin-regulation**: Read before you analyze ATAC-seq, ChIP-seq, CUT&Tag, or scATAC data from peaks, counts, or signal tracks. It gives differential binding, peak annotation, and signal profiles, the upstream facts that change the counts, and which motif analyses are not available. ([inflexa-ai/inflexa](https://github.com/inflexa-ai/inflexa/tree/HEAD/skills/chromatin-regulation) · ★38)
- **chromatin-rna-fraction-enrichment-quantification**: Use when a native Nanopore dRNA-seq sample (e.g., chromatin-fraction RNA from K562 cells, GEO GSM4663623) must be split into premature vs mature reads to verify subcellular-fraction enrichment of unprocessed RNA. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/chromatin-rna-fraction-enrichment-quantification))
- **chromatin-state-annotation**: Use when you have genome-wide multi-track epigenomic signals (histone mark ChIP-seq tracks) for one or more Roadmap/ENCODE samples and need discrete chromatin state sequences as input to EpiAlign alignment or compression benchmarking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-state-annotation))
- **chromatin-state-frequency-vector-comparison**: Use when benchmarking an order-aware chromatin-state comparison method (e.g. EpiAlign) against a simple, order-agnostic baseline, or when only overall chromatin state composition — not sequence order — is needed to distinguish epigenomic samples. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-state-frequency-vector-comparison))
- **chromatin-state-inference**: This skill should be used when users need to infer chromatin states from histone modification ChIP-seq data using chromHMM. It provides workflows for chromatin state segmentation, model training, state annotation. ([diegosouzapw/awesome-omni-skill](https://github.com/diegosouzapw/awesome-omni-skill/tree/HEAD/skills/data-ai/chromatin-state-inference-majiayu000) · ★67 · 6 copies)
- **chromatin-state-regulatory-element-classification**: Use when RNAPII ChIA-PET loop calls (PET count ≥3 with peak support) need to be annotated as enhancer-promoter or promoter-promoter interactions and exported as separate per-category loop files for visualization in BASIC Browser tracks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-state-regulatory-element-classification))
- **chromatin-state-sequence-compression**: Use when chromatin state sequences (e.g. from ChromHMM on Roadmap epigenomes) are about to be aligned with EpiAlign, or when uncompressed sequences contain long runs of a single state such as the quiescent state 'o'. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-state-sequence-compression))
- **chromatin-state-weight-scoring**: Use when two genomic regions are represented as ChromHMM-derived chromatin state label sequences and you need a similarity score that accounts for varying state lengths, state frequencies, mismatches, and deletions (e.g. -match queries in 500 kb regions). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatin-state-weight-scoring))
- **chromatogram-aird-compression**: Use when a workflow needs to shrink large MRM raw datasets (e.g. PXD009543, PXD031038, Skyline test set) for web-based rendering or storage, or when benchmarking file-format compression ratios. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-aird-compression))
- **chromatogram-alignment-warping**: Use when you have baseline-corrected and smoothed 2D-TIC chromatogram objects from individual GCxGC-MS samples that exhibit retention-time variations relative to a reference chromatogram, and you need to align peak positions across both dimensions before joining multiple samples for multiway PCA or. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-alignment-warping) · 2 copies)
- **chromatogram-and-ionization-intensity-modeling**: Use when when you need to simulate LC-MS/MS data for fragmentation strategy development and do not have (or wish to augment) real experimental chromatograms. Specifically: (1) you have a list of known or sampled chemical compounds with molecular formulae; ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-and-ionization-intensity-modeling) · 3 copies)
- **chromatogram-artifact-removal**: Use when raw NetCDF-format GCxGC-MS chromatograms exhibit steady or increasing baseline intensity caused by instrumental contamination, column bleeding, or thermal drift; ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-artifact-removal) · 2 copies)
- **chromatogram-baseline-correction-preprocessing**: Use when you have raw or folded 2D-TIC chromatogram data (typically imported from NetCDF files into RGCxGC chromatogram objects) that exhibits baseline drift, chemical noise, or instrumental artifacts that would obscure true metabolite peaks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-baseline-correction-preprocessing) · 2 copies)
- **chromatogram-binning-and-noise-level-estimation**: Use when processing centroided high-resolution LC-MS mzML files where you need to convert sparse MS1 spectra into continuous chromatographic mass tracks and quantify baseline noise to gate smoothing and peak-detection thresholds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-binning-and-noise-level-estimation))
- **chromatogram-cdf-file-import**: Use when raw GCxGC-MS acquisition data are stored as NetCDF/CDF files (e.g. 08GB.cdf, 09GB.cdf from MetaboLights MTBLS579) and must be converted into RGCxGC 2D-TIC chromatogram objects before preprocessing or multivariate analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-cdf-file-import))
- **chromatogram-cosine-similarity-optimization**: Use when MRM/LC-MS samples show retention time shifts that break peak correspondence across EICs and you need per-sample drift estimates to align peaks or group EICs across runs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-cosine-similarity-optimization))
- **chromatogram-cross-correlation-rt-shift-estimation**: Use when preprocessing a batch of mzML MRM chromatograms (quantifier + qualifier traces) whose peak RTs drift between samples and need alignment before peak integration, e.g. HILIC 1a-style demodata batches in automRm. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-cross-correlation-rt-shift-estimation))
- **chromatogram-deconvolution**: Use when raw LC-HRMS2 chromatograms (converted from Thermo RAW via MSConvert) contain co-eluting or partially resolved peaks that the ADAP chromatogram builder grouped into a single chromatogram, and distinct peak boundaries must be recovered before MS2 export. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-deconvolution--6e1356a2fa1ea462))
- **chromatogram-deconvolution**: Use when you have MS1-only LC/HRMS data (mzXML, mzML, netCDF) from untargeted metabolomics and need to deconvolve co-eluting MS1 ions into composite spectra without DDA MS2 files. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-deconvolution--cc62ef47a69ecb5e))
- **chromatogram-extracted-ion-generation**: Use when starting from raw mzXML/mzML LC-MS files of chemical standards (e.g., HILIC metabolomics replicates) and per-analyte EICs are needed for peak quality metrics such as MCQ, asymmetry factor, FWHM, or Std/Blk ratio. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-extracted-ion-generation))
- **chromatogram-extraction-and-visualization**: Use when you have sqMass files containing pre-extracted transition group chromatograms from DIA-MS experiments and need to interactively visualize individual peptide precursor chromatograms, inspect peak quality via Q-values (typically 1% FDR cutoff), and overlay peak boundaries or apply on-the-fly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-extraction-and-visualization) · 2 copies)
- **chromatogram-extraction-from-parquet-ms-data**: Use when a chromatogram (TIC, BPI, or EIC for a specific m/z window) must be computed from an on-disk Parquet HRMS file too large to load fully into memory (e.g. ~1.9 Gb in-RAM, 200-400 MB Parquet profile data). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-extraction-from-parquet-ms-data))
- **chromatogram-import-and-processing-in-ms-dial**: Use when raw centroid-mode LC–MS all-ion-fragmentation chromatograms need feature detection and pseudo-MS/MS deconvolution to produce a comparable annotation baseline (e.g. against MetaboAnnotatoR rank-1 or manual expert lists) on datasets like Lipid+, Lipid−, or HILIC+. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-import-and-processing-in-ms-dial))
- **chromatogram-netcdf-file-import**: Use when you have raw GCxGC-MS chromatogram data in NetCDF (CDF) format from an instrument and need to load it into R for preprocessing (smoothing, baseline correction, peak alignment) and multivariate analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-netcdf-file-import) · 2 copies)
- **chromatogram-netcdf-import**: Use when the starting point of a GCxGC-MS analysis is a raw instrument chromatogram stored in NetCDF (e.g. a .cdf file) and it must be converted into a folded 2D-TIC object before preprocessing or multivariate analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-netcdf-import))
- **chromatogram-peak-alignment-2dcow**: Use when a smoothed, baseline-corrected two-dimensional chromatogram (2D-TIC) must be warped pixel-wise onto a reference chromatogram of the same dataset to remove retention-time shifts before multivariate analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-peak-alignment-2dcow))
- **chromatogram-peak-boundary-detection**: Use when when you have loaded a TransitionGroup (extracted ion chromatogram or mobilogram from DIA-MS data) and need to identify precise peak boundaries and apex positions for feature extraction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-peak-boundary-detection) · 3 copies)
- **chromatogram-peak-height-quantification**: Use when you have .ab1 Sanger trace files plus a reference .fasta from a bisulfite sequencing PCR experiment and need per-CpG methylation percentages computed from dye peak heights. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-peak-height-quantification))
- **chromatogram-peak-matching-registration**: Use when you have multiple LC-MS samples in mzML or mzXML format with variable retention times, at least one designated as a quality control (QC) reference file, and you need to correct for RT drift before peak/feature detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-peak-matching-registration) · 2 copies)
- **chromatogram-plot-generation-retention-time**: Use when you have mass spectrometry data loaded as a Pandas DataFrame with retention time and intensity columns, and you need to visualize the overall or mass-trace-specific signal intensity distribution across the chromatographic separation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-plot-generation-retention-time) · 4 copies)
- **chromatogram-plotting-from-massql-results**: Use when MassQL queries have been run over a directory of mzML files (e.g., SST deuterated acylcarnitine standards on a UHPLC-HRMS) and the peak/scan results need to be visualized as EICs, Gaussian fits, and MS2 plots and exported as tables and PDF reports. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-plotting-from-massql-results))
- **chromatogram-quality-label-comparison**: Use when you have a feature table plus .mzXML/.mzML raw files, a CNN model (EVA/pyEVA) producing predicted EIC quality classes, and manually annotated good/poor labels against which classification accuracy must be measured. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-quality-label-comparison))
- **chromatogram-retention-time-visualization**: Use when you have mass spectrometry data loaded as a pandas DataFrame with at minimum two numeric columns representing retention time and intensity values, and you need to generate a chromatogram visualization for exploratory analysis, quality control, or publication. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-retention-time-visualization))
- **chromatogram-smoothing-parameter-tuning**: Use when building an in-house LC-MS RT/m/z library with metScribeR and EIC peaks (from mzML files of authentic standards) show poorly assigned start/end boundaries or noisy baselines, requiring tuning of smoothing and density-filter settings before applying peak-finding thresholds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-smoothing-parameter-tuning))
- **chromatogram-smoothing-similarity-analysis**: Use when an LC/MS EIC must be scored for quality via smoothing- and background-similarity measures, or when benchmarking a comparator EIC metric against a proposed alternative (e.g. the global zigzag index). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-smoothing-similarity-analysis))
- **chromatogram-tic-xic-bpc-computation**: Use when the input is a proprietary Thermo Fisher Scientific .raw file and the analysis goal is TIC/XIC/BPC traces from within R, especially to avoid ProteoWizard/mzML conversion steps. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-tic-xic-bpc-computation))
- **chromatogram-total-ion-current-aggregation**: Use when you have a columnar Parquet HRMS file with rt, intensity and mslevel columns and need a TIC (intensity summed per retention time for MS1 scans) without loading the whole file into memory. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-total-ion-current-aggregation))
- **chromatogram-trace-plotting**: Use when you have retention-time and intensity arrays extracted from an MZA (HDF5-based) file with mzapy (e.g., via get_tic or collect_xic_arrays_by_mz) and need a visual chromatogram saved as an image like PNG. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-trace-plotting))
- **chromatogram-trace-quality-trimming**: Use when raw .ab1 Sanger sequencing trace files must be trimmed of low-quality and mixed-base ends prior to computing CpG methylation percentages from direct-BSP or cloning-BSP data, or when a final trimmed sequence is too short and trimming thresholds need adjustment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-trace-quality-trimming))
- **chromatogram-trace-reconstruction**: Use when you have an mzML file (e.g. a Beer_multibeers_1_T10_POS.mzML-style acquisition) and want to derive the chemical list for a ViMMS simulation from its real peaks rather than sampling synthetic chemicals from HMDB. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-trace-reconstruction))
- **chromatogram-visualization-generation**: Use when after importing mass spectrometry data in .raw, .d, or mzXML format into R and before peak analysis or quality control steps. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatogram-visualization-generation))
- **chromatogram-xic-extraction-from-raw-files**: Use when the input is a Thermo Fisher Orbitap .raw file (e.g., 20181113_010_autoQC01.raw) and you need chromatographic traces/XICs for target masses in R, e.g., to derive retention times for iRT peptides. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatogram-xic-extraction-from-raw-files))
- **chromatographic-alignment**: Align chromatographic peaks across LC-MS samples with retention-time correction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skill-collections](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skill-collections/tree/HEAD/tests/fixtures/router_collection/leaves/chromatographic-alignment) · ★16)
- **chromatographic-alignment-parameter-selection**: Use when after chromatographic peak detection (e.g. centWave) has been performed on LC-MS data and you need to group features that likely originate from the same compound. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-alignment-parameter-selection) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-alignment-tuning**: Use when when processing a cohort of centroided mzML LC-MS files with high sample-to-sample retention time and m/z drift, and you need reproducible alignment of detected peaks across all samples before gap-filling and feature consolidation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-alignment-tuning) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-anomaly-diagnosis**: Use when longitudinal LC-MS/MS SST data (e.g., deuterated acylcarnitine standards in mzML) must be screened for retention time drift, column-lot shifts, or wrong chromatographic method use prior to experimental acquisition. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-anomaly-diagnosis))
- **chromatographic-auto-correlation-noise-filtering**: Use when raw LC/MS data in mzML format must be converted into an MS1 ion-feature table without manually tuned detection thresholds, and noise must be separated from true chromatographic peaks in a data-driven, parameter-free way. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-auto-correlation-noise-filtering))
- **chromatographic-background-signal-modeling**: Reference for modeling non-peak background signals (e.g. walls spanning large chromatogram parts) when generating augmented training instances for peak-picking CNN training in LC-HRMS. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-background-signal-modeling))
- **chromatographic-baseline-correction**: Use when raw LC-MS EICs contain baseline drift that would distort peak-position detection or peak-area integration of targeted metabolites and their isotopologues, prior to MassSpecWavelet peak picking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-baseline-correction))
- **chromatographic-baseline-estimation**: Use when you have extracted ion chromatogram (EIC) candidate data from untargeted LC/HRMS files (mzXML, mzML, or netCDF format) and need to identify genuine peaks within each EIC. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-baseline-estimation))
- **chromatographic-baseline-estimation**: Use when processing acoustic ejection MS MRM data where per-ejection peak areas must be baseline-corrected before quantification, e.g. to compute A(m,k) transition areas from rtmsEcho-extracted XICs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-baseline-estimation))
- **chromatographic-batch-variation-assessment**: Use when LC-MS/MS samples span two chromatographic batches (a metadata 'batch' column) and you need to test whether chemical-hierarchy-based beta diversity mitigates the batch effect versus an unweighted feature-table distance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-batch-variation-assessment))
- **chromatographic-calibration-curve-fitting**: Use when comparing quantification accuracy of two MRM analysis tools (e.g., MRMPro vs. Skyline) on a shared multi-file dataset with defined transitions, where peak areas can be regressed against known concentrations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-calibration-curve-fitting))
- **chromatographic-coelution-detection**: Use when after feature detection when you have a feature table with m/z, retention time, and intensity columns, and you need to group features into empirical compounds (putative metabolites) that account for isotopologue patterns and multiple adduct forms arising from a single underlying analyte. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-coelution-detection) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-column-scaling-analysis**: Use when measured HILIC RTs come from a modified gradient or a shorter column (100/50/30 mm Kinetex HILIC) and must be mapped onto the reference database RT scale before lipid identification by retention time. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-column-scaling-analysis))
- **chromatographic-data-integration-qc**: Use when you need batch-consistent peak areas/intensities per feature across GC×GC–MS samples (e.g. a serial dilution QC series) and must verify quantitative agreement with gold-standard software like ChromaTOF. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-data-integration-qc))
- **chromatographic-data-storage-comparison**: Use when comparing mass spectrometry data conversion pipelines or storage formats (e.g., Parquet vs mzML/mz5/mzMLb) on conversion time, file size, and data read/query speed for HRMS or IMS-HDMS^E datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-data-storage-comparison))
- **chromatographic-data-structure-abstraction**: Use when when ingesting raw mass spectrometry data from multiple instrument vendors or file formats into a metabolomics processing pipeline, and you need to expose spectral and chromatographic metadata through a single, consistent interface regardless of the source format's internal structure. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-data-structure-abstraction) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-data-structuring**: Use when after parsing a centroid mzML file into (m/z, scan_number, intensity) tuples, when you need to organize sparse MS1 data for efficient peak detection and cross-sample alignment. Apply this skill when high mass resolution (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-data-structuring))
- **chromatographic-data-visualization**: Use when after peak detection has been completed and a feature table has been generated in MetCohort. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-data-visualization) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-eic-extraction**: Use when raw LC–MS mzML files must yield EICs for specific target compounds — e.g., extracting chromatograms for the five system suitability solution standards (leucine enkephaline, lysophosphatidylcholine (0:0/18:0), L-phenylalanyl-L-phenylalanine, L-tryptophan, alogliptin) from QC injections. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-eic-extraction))
- **chromatographic-eic-visualization**: Use when targeted LC–MS metabolomics/lipidomics data (mzML raw runs) must be sanity-checked before peak integration — e.g., verifying that target peaks are visible within expected m/z and RT windows in QC runs prior to running full TARDIS integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-eic-visualization))
- **chromatographic-elution-order-comparison**: Use when predicted retention times from one chromatographic method (e.g., SMRT reverse-phase) must be made comparable to RTs measured under other experimental methods (e.g., PredRet ECMs), and median absolute projection errors are needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-elution-order-comparison))
- **chromatographic-error-window-coverage-calculation**: Use when you have trained retention-time prediction models (e.g. Retip's fit.rf, fit.keras, fit.xgboost, fit.lightgbm, fit.brnn) and experimental RT values for test/validation compounds, and need to quantify what fraction of true positives fall within a fixed RT error window. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-error-window-coverage-calculation))
- **chromatographic-feature-correlation-clustering**: Use when input is MS1-only mzML/mzXML (or imzML) data lacking tandem spectra and the goal is to assemble in-source fragments correlated with a precursor into pseudo MS/MS spectra for structural annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-correlation-clustering))
- **chromatographic-feature-correlation-filtering**: Use when you have a chromatographic peak table from LC-MS lipidomics (e.g. Compound Discoverer output) with identified lipid features and numerous unidentified features, and you want to filter out co-eluting spurious/redundant features before downstream statistics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-correlation-filtering))
- **chromatographic-feature-extraction**: Use when you have centroided mzML files from LC- or GC-HRMS instruments (acquired in data-dependent mode with ddMS2) and need to systematically identify chromatographic peaks, measure their mass and retention time coordinates, and quantify their intensities before applying PFAS-specific. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-feature-extraction) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-feature-matching**: Use when two peak-detection tools have been run on the same LC-HRMS samples and you must match their detected features, compare peak areas, and quantify correlation (R2) and systematic area-scaling factors. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-matching))
- **chromatographic-feature-metadata-extraction**: Reference for extracting per-feature metadata such as peak_area and calibration/IS metavalues from FeatureDB reports in targeted metabolomics workflows before fitting calibration curves or computing quantitation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-metadata-extraction))
- **chromatographic-feature-refinement**: Use when after performing retention-time-based grouping on LC-MS data (e.g., with a 10–20 second window) and observing that many feature groups contain features with similar retention times but dissimilar abundance patterns across samples or different extracted ion chromatogram peak shapes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-feature-refinement) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-feature-table-construction**: Use when starting from raw LC-MS/MS MS1 scans (mzML or Bruker .d) and a feature table with complete signal coverage is needed before adduct grouping, alignment, or annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-feature-table-construction))
- **chromatographic-fwhm-overlap-scoring**: Use when assembling LC-MS mass traces into metabolite isotope features and you must verify that candidate isotope traces co-elute with the monoisotopic trace before accepting a feature hypothesis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-fwhm-overlap-scoring))
- **chromatographic-gradient-evaluation**: Use when raw LC-MS (mzML/mzXML) data from a gradient run must be reduced to a single separation-performance number (GSI) to evaluate or optimize a chromatographic gradient. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-gradient-evaluation))
- **chromatographic-gradient-parameterization**: Use when you must define the candidate LC-MS gradient space before fitting the GP model: you have gradient-definition parameters (dimensionality, time points, mobile-phase settings) and need the constrained gridX/scaledX candidate set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-gradient-parameterization))
- **chromatographic-ion-clusterisation**: Use when analysing HPTLC coupled with high-resolution mass spectrometry data (e.g. mzXML-derived extracted ion chromatograms) and you need to partition ions of interest from background ions before structure elucidation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-ion-clusterisation))
- **chromatographic-local-maxima-detection**: Use when processing raw profile-mode LC-HRMS chromatograms (mzXML/mzML) and you need to enumerate all candidate peak-apex signals before machine-learning peak/background classification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-local-maxima-detection))
- **chromatographic-method-transfer**: Use when you have retention time predictions from a source chromatographic method and need to predict retention times for a target chromatographic method, but have limited calibration data (10–100 molecules) measured on both methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v1/skills/chromatographic-method-transfer) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-method-transfer-learning**: Use when you have experimental RT measurements from a source chromatographic method and need to predict RTs for the same molecules on a target chromatographic method, but lack a large calibration dataset (typical scenario: 10–100 molecules with ground truth RTs on both methods). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-method-transfer-learning) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-misalignment-detection**: Use when after running XCMS-based alignment on LC-MS datasets with hundreds of samples or data acquisition periods longer than a week, when the assumption that all m/z bins in the same sample share a single warping function is likely to fail. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-misalignment-detection) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-modality-classification**: Use when when ingesting raw or vendor-format mass spectrometry data files of unknown or mixed acquisition modality, and you need to automatically determine whether the input originated from liquid chromatography (LC), gas chromatography (GC), ion mobility spectrometry (IMS), or MS imaging (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-modality-classification) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-model-peak-selection**: Use when SWATH-MS data have multiplexed MS/MS spectra in which fragment ions co-elute and chromatographic peaks are unresolved, and metabolite-specific MS2 spectra must be reconstructed for downstream annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-model-peak-selection))
- **chromatographic-modulation-folding**: Use when raw comprehensive two-dimensional GC–MS data in CDF files must be folded into a 4D tensor (samples, m/z fragments, second-dimension and first-dimension retention time) as the entry point of an untargeted GC×GC–MS workflow. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-modulation-folding))
- **chromatographic-noise-modeling**: Use when you need to generate blank or background-only .mzML files for method validation, when you want to create synthetic negative controls with realistic instrumental noise but no analyte peaks, or when you need to simulate serum matrix background (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-noise-modeling) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-alignment-input-preparation**: Use when raw LC/HRMS data (mzXML, mzML, netCDF) must be converted into chromatographic m/z-RT peaklists and an aligned peak table before running the CSA, DDA, or DIA deconvolution workflows of IDSL.CSA. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-alignment-input-preparation))
- **chromatographic-peak-alignment-qc**: Use when GC/MS chromatograms have already been deconvolved with eRah and aligned compound tables contain gaps (compounds found in some samples but not others) that must be filled before quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-alignment-qc))
- **chromatographic-peak-annotation-visualization**: Use when after running tardisPeaks() with screening_mode=TRUE on centroided .mzML LC-MS data, when you need to visually inspect whether the 10 target compounds (internal standards and endogenous metabolites) were correctly detected within their expected m/z and retention time windows. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-annotation-visualization) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-area-integration**: Use when processing targeted MS data where peak detection has produced EICs (after airPLS baseline correction and MassSpecWavelet peak positioning) and per-sample peak areas must be computed for a chosen isotopologue or metabolite. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-area-integration))
- **chromatographic-peak-baseline-reconstruction**: Use when MRM ion chromatograms contain noisy peaks, unevenly sampled signals, or coeluted isomers where connecting nearby valleys (e.g., TargetLynx) misestimates the baseline; adaptive section-wise background reconstruction is needed before peak integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-baseline-reconstruction))
- **chromatographic-peak-boundary-correction**: Use when refining the start/stop limits of peaks detected in smoothed CE-MS or HILIC-MS ROI chromatograms (intensity-vs-retention-time traces), after CWT/Mexican-hat peak-center detection but before peak integration of RT, area, and height. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-boundary-correction))
- **chromatographic-peak-boundary-detection**: Use when reconstructing chromatographic peak boundaries from centroided LC-MS data — e.g. when a growing mass trace spans several valleys and must be split so each trace corresponds to one chromatographic peak, or when apex-based boundary definition is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-boundary-detection))
- **chromatographic-peak-characterization**: Use when after screening-mode validation of your m/z and retention time (RT) windows has confirmed that targets are visible in your data. Use it when you have centroided .mzML files, a validated target compound table with adjusted expected RT values (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-characterization) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-classification**: Use when you have (1) a benchmark dataset of reference peaks with validated m/z, retention time boundaries, and isotopologue assignments, and (2) NPP output feature tables (unaligned and aligned) from tools like XCMS, MZmine 2, or MS-DIAL that you wish to evaluate. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-classification))
- **chromatographic-peak-detection**: Use when raw LC-MS targeted metabolomics EICs (e.g. centWave-exported chromatograms such as the MM14 or human blood datasets) need peak positions, bounds and areas quantified per compound, especially when multiple candidate peaks must be resolved to the true M+H/M+Na/M+K isotopologue peak. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection--5fea644988e8baaa))
- **chromatographic-peak-detection**: Use when MRM-mode LC-QQQ-MS raw data (mzML) must be preprocessed into peak intensity tables without manual review, e.g. batches from instruments like an Agilent 6495 QQQ-MS needing automatic peak picking and quality control. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection--ff4f929bb4f7747e))
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-and-grouping**: Use when when you have processed LC-MS data through XCMS alignment but suspect misaligned features due to retention-time drift over long acquisition periods (&gt;1 week) or large sample batches (hundreds of samples), or when peak-filling produces unexpectedly high coefficient-of-variation (CV &gt; 0. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-and-grouping) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-and-integration**: Use when after loading centroided .mzML LC-MS data and defining a target list (compound ID, name, m/z, RT, polarity) when you need to: (1) automatically locate and integrate peaks for known compounds across multiple runs; (2) generate per-target and per-run quantitative metrics; ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-and-integration) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-and-segmentation**: Use when you have raw LC-MS data (mzML or vendor format) and need to discover and characterize all chromatographic features present, without prior knowledge of target analytes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-and-segmentation) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-gradient-descent**: Use when you have LC-HRMS profile-mode data (retention time × m/z matrix format) and need to automatically identify chromatographic peak locations and boundaries prior to feature extraction, reference matching, or training a peak-classification CNN model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-gradient-descent))
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-msw**: Use when you have raw mzML files from an FTICR-MS or other direct-injection MS instrument and need to identify discrete chromatographic peaks across the m/z and retention-time dimensions. Use this skill when you must isolate individual ion signals before applying calibration corrections (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-msw) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-retention-time-alignment**: Use when you have multiple high-resolution mzML files from LC-MS/MS experiments (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-retention-time-alignment) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-validation**: Use when you have centroided mzML data and a curated set of target molecules with known retention time (RT) boundaries, and you need to establish ground-truth peak detection performance metrics (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-validation))
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-wavelet**: Use when you have loaded raw LC-MS or direct-injection FTICR-MS data (in mzML or netCDF format) into an XCMSnExp object and need to identify individual chromatographic peaks before feature grouping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-wavelet) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-detection-with-prominence-control**: Use when after constructing baseline-corrected mass tracks (either composite across samples or per-sample) when you need to identify individual chromatographic peaks for feature extraction in LC-MS or GC-MS metabolomics workflows. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-detection-with-prominence-control) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-edge-detection**: Use when processing raw LC-MS ROI intensity arrays (e.g. mzML or Bruker TDF2 .d via MassCube) and you need to split each mass trace into discrete chromatographic peak segments bounded by start/end edge indices. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-edge-detection))
- **chromatographic-peak-extraction-and-retention-time-filtering**: Use when when you have LC-MS/MS raw data (mzML or netCDF format) and need to isolate a specific compound's signal based on its known or suspected m/z value and retention time range. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-extraction-and-retention-time-filtering) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-fwhm-window-grouping**: Use when an aligned XCMS feature table (m/z, retention time, apex intensity, per-sample intensities) must be partitioned into co-eluting compound spectra as the first CAMERA grouping step before isotope/adduct annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-fwhm-window-grouping))
- **chromatographic-peak-integration-rt-area-height**: Use when untargeted metabolomics ROIs (intensity-vs-retention-time traces from CE-MS or HILIC-MS runs, e.g. .mzML/.mzXML converted profiles) must be converted into a per-peak table of apex RT, area, and height for feature integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-integration-rt-area-height))
- **chromatographic-peak-isolation-and-refinement**: Use when after sliding-window analysis has identified candidate TIC peaks but before extracting chromatographic parameters (retention time, peak width, intensity) from extracted ion chromatograms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-isolation-and-refinement))
- **chromatographic-peak-labeling**: Use when you have a user-defined reference list of isolated, high-confidence chromatographic peaks (ground-truth) matched across multiple LC-HRMS samples, and you need to produce a labelled training dataset large enough to train a CNN model that discriminates true peaks from background noise and. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-labeling))
- **chromatographic-peak-ms2-spectra-extraction**: Use when you have an xcms result object with centWave-detected chromatographic peaks (e.g. from an mzML LC-MS/MS run like HighIS_Mix07_CE20_POS.mzML) and need the tandem MS2 spectra behind those peaks to compare against a reference library such as an HMDB 5.0 CompDb. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-ms2-spectra-extraction))
- **chromatographic-peak-overlap-resolution**: Use when analyzing GC-MS data containing overlapping chromatographic peaks—a common scenario in untargeted metabolomics and environmental screening where sample complexity or chromatographic resolution limitations cause co-elution of structurally similar or temporally proximate compounds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-overlap-resolution) · 4 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-picking**: Use when starting from raw HRMS .mzML files and you need to extract chromatographic features (m/z–RT pairs) for non-targeted analysis, e.g. ENTACT-style mixtures or environmental water samples. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-picking--2cc39905d492bb0b))
- **chromatographic-peak-picking**: Use when starting from raw MS data files (e.g. LC-MS runs) and a feature-level peak list is needed as the basis for deisotoping, alignment, and gap filling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-picking--84ee8dab25694863))
- **chromatographic-peak-picking**: Use when raw LC-MS mzXML files (any vendor format compatible with MS-Convert) need MS1 peak picking to build a JPA feature table, or as the PP baseline step before MR and targeted feature extraction in metabolomics/exposomics processing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-picking--c371cbed79b4941f))
- **chromatographic-peak-processing**: Use when after peak detection when you have a table of detected peaks with m/z values and retention times from LC/HRMS data, and you observe systematic m/z drift across a batch or population-scale study (n &gt; 500 samples). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-processing))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment**: Use when you have targeted/suspect HRMS data (mzML) plus a compound or mass list and need per-peak QC (MS1/MS2 existence, noise, RT shift) before further identification or spectral upload. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment--9af6849a84c39e4f))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment**: Use when when you have a feature table (CSV with m/z and retention time columns) and corresponding LC-MS raw data files (.mzXML or .mzML) from an untargeted metabolomics experiment, and need to filter out spurious peaks before downstream statistical or biological interpretation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment**: Use when extracted ion chromatograms (EICs) from LC/MS metabolomics profiles have already been peak-detected and individual peaks (or EICs) must be scored, filtered, or classified as good vs. bad before downstream processing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-assessment--577231b7ebfad399))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering**: Use when raw centroid/profile HRMS scans (.mzML) have been peak-picked and the feature list must be cleaned of low-quality peaks — split peaks, peaks spanning too few scans, and high-baseline-noise features — before annotation or statistical analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering--e10fb7f7afcb7891))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering**: Use when processing LC-MS mzML files of authentic metabolite standards and you need to find peaks on adduct EICs and remove low-quality detections before assigning retention times and m/z for an in-house reference library. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering--513a3253f15d053d))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering**: Use when processing MS1-level LC/HRMS peak candidates (e.g. from IDSL.IPA EIC detection) and you need to discard low-quality or redundant isotope peaks using the seven-parameter peak quality filter before building aligned data matrices. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-filtering--7ffa5de193723c9b))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-computation**: Use when you have an XCMS pre-processed untargeted LC–MS dataset (getEIC + fillPeaks output) and need numeric per-peak quality features to train or apply a Pass/Fail peak-quality classifier. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-computation--8df5c515366f3394))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-computation**: Use when LC/MS chromatographic peaks have been detected on extracted ion chromatograms (EICs) and you need per-peak numeric quality scores to filter 'bad' peaks before downstream metabolomics processing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-computation--506f40a4b68c44fd))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-evaluation**: Use when when processing untargeted LC-MS metabolomics data with XCMS and need to identify low-quality peak integrations that may introduce noise or bias into subsequent compound identification and quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-metric-evaluation) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-metrics**: Use when evaluating LC-MS metabolomics peak quality for chemical standards: you have EICs from mzXML/mzML files (with optional replicate and blank runs) and need standardized per-peak quality metrics to validate or rank peaks. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-metrics))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-score-computation**: Use when preprocessing LC-QQQ-MS raw data in MRM mode and you need per-candidate peak quality metrics (shape, quantifier/qualifier coelution, RT deviation) before ML-based peak reporting. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-score-computation))
- **chromatographic-peak-quality-scoring**: Use when raw MRM-mode LC-MS data (mzML with quantifier plus qualifier transitions and expected RTs in metabdb.xlsx) must be converted into a trustworthy set of reported peaks, i.e. deciding which of several peak candidates per metabolite/sample is the correct one. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-quality-scoring))
- **chromatographic-peak-r13c-computation**: Reference for quantifying the 12C/13C isotope-pair intensity ratio of individual chromatographic peaks and correlating it with molecular formulas or predicted carbon number. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-r13c-computation))
- **chromatographic-peak-resolution**: Use when when you have loaded raw GC-MS data in netCDF or mzML format and visual or statistical inspection reveals overlapping chromatographic peaks (i.e., multiple m/z ions co-eluting at the same retention time window). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-resolution) · 2 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-accuracy-benchmarking**: Use when a peak-detection algorithm needs quantitative accuracy assessment against ground-truth manually labeled EIC peak-shape classes (double vs single peak) on experimental LC-MS data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-accuracy-benchmarking))
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-analysis**: Use when raw CE-MS or LC-MS data (converted to .mzXML/.mzML via msconvert) has been peak-detected with the CWT/ROI search step and noise/false-positive peaks must be culled by shape before feature integration, scaling, and mid-level data fusion. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-analysis))
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-comparison**: Use when after retention-time-based and abundance-correlation-based feature grouping have produced composite feature groups, and you need to identify which features within a group actually represent different compounds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-comparison) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-correlation**: Use when processing untargeted LC-MS raw data (mzML or Bruker .d) where co-eluting ions must be distinguished as genuine metabolites versus in-source fragments or alternative adducts before quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-correlation))
- **chromatographic-peak-shape-correlation-analysis**: Decision support for splitting CAMERA pseudospectra that the retention-time window alone left ambiguous — i.e. when features co-elute within one FWHM group but their EIC peak shapes (corval, pval thresholds on calcCaS edges) indicate two overlapping analytes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-shape-correlation-analysis))
- **chromatographic-peak-smoothing-and-noise-estimation**: Use when performing semi-targeted LC-MS peak quantification on converted Aird data (e.g. TripleTOF or QE HF acquisitions) and a smoothing or noise-estimation strategy must be selected among MetaPro's 4 smoothing and 3 noise-estimation options before peak selection and integration. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-smoothing-and-noise-estimation))
- **chromatographic-peak-snr-filtering**: Use when filtering candidate peaks detected from GC×GC–MS 4D intensity arrays (CDF-derived GcDuo objects) by signal-to-noise and peak-shape criteria before deconvolution, to discard false positives such as incomplete or sub-threshold signals. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-snr-filtering))
- **chromatographic-peak-tip-detection**: Use when processing high-throughput LC-MS/MS MRM ion chromatograms where peaks may be coeluted, noisy, or unevenly sampled and per-transition peak tips, valleys, and coelution flags must be resolved before quantitation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-tip-detection))
- **chromatographic-peak-tracking-for-user-defined-compounds**: Use when you have raw untargeted metabolomics files (mzML/mzXML/mzData) plus a user-defined list of m/z and RT pairs for QC or contaminant compounds, and need to track observed m/z, retention time, intensity, and peak shape over time or batches. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-tracking-for-user-defined-compounds))
- **chromatographic-peak-validation**: Use when replicate LC/MS injections of chemical standard mixtures (mzXML/mzML with blank runs) must be filtered into validated peaks to build a retention-time reference library, e.g. the HILIC LC-MS platform's 571-metabolite library. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-validation))
- **chromatographic-peak-vs-background-classification**: Use when you have profile-mode LC-HRMS chromatograms and a user-defined reference-feature list (ground truth) and need to distinguish true chromatographic peaks from background/walls with a custom-trained CNN. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-vs-background-classification))
- **chromatographic-peak-width-measurement**: Use when you need to set chromatographic peak-width parameters for an LC-MS feature extraction run (e.g. XCMS CentWave peak width, MS-DIAL tolerances) from raw untargeted metabolomics data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-width-measurement))
- **chromatographic-peak-width-tuning**: Use when configuring eRah deconvolveComp() on GC-EI MS chromatograms (netCDF/mzXML) and you must set min.peak.width, min.peak.height, noise.threshold, or avoid.processing.mz before running CMLC/OSD. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-peak-width-tuning))
- **chromatographic-profile-quality-assessment**: Use when running targeted peak detection on LC-MS data acquired with multiple overlapping m/z scan windows and observing distorted or periodically discontinuous peak profiles in EIC plots. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/chromatographic-profile-quality-assessment) · 3 copies)
- **chromatographic-quality-metric-calculation**: Use when targeted LC–MS peak integration (e.g., TARDIS on raw metabolomics/lipidomics runs) has produced per-target peak tables and you need quantitative quality metrics to filter poor-quality peaks or validate QC-run clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-quality-metric-calculation))
- **chromatographic-quality-metric-implementation**: Use when you must programmatically score the quality of extracted ion chromatograms (EICs) or detected chromatographic peaks in LC/MS metabolomics data — especially EICs with high background signals or containing only a single peak — before downstream peak detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/chromatographic-quality-metric-implementation))
